2  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
上週重要市場資訊
⚫ 美國 9 月通膨數據高於市場預期，CPI 月增 0.6%、年增 3.7%；核心 CPI 月增
0.3%、年增 2.6%。同月 PPI 月增 1.3%，創 2022 年 3 月以來最大增幅，年增
6.3%，顯示通膨壓力已逐步由能源價格擴散至更廣泛領域。數據公布後，市場一
度提高對聯準會 10 月升息的預期，但隨後多位官員釋出審慎訊號，使升息押注
降溫。聯準會理事鮑曼表示，決策前仍有時間觀察先前升息效果；舊金山聯準銀
行總裁戴莉則認為 10 月無立即升息之必要。而褐皮書顯示，美國就業市場維持
「低招聘、低裁員」格局，而高能源價格帶來的通膨壓力仍值得關注。
⚫ 中國 9 月通膨持續回升，CPI 年增 0.9%，創近三個月高點；PPI 年增 5.0%，創
逾四年最大漲幅，顯示通縮壓力進一步減輕。主要受能源與原物料價格上漲帶
動，且 9 月進出口表現優於預期，AI 產業鏈需求與貿易環境改善提供支撐；不

過，信貸增長仍仰賴政府發債與季底效應，內需與民間融資動能仍偏弱。
⚫ 日本銀行審議委員小枝淳子表示，不宜對升息過於保守，並認為政策利率仍有進
一步上調空間。總裁植田和男則強調維持 2%通膨目標的重要性。整體而言，日
本央行持續釋出偏鷹派訊號，有助支撐日圓表現。
⚫ 歐洲央行管委溫施表示，在能源價格波動下，為了控制通膨仍不排除進一步升息
可能，總裁拉加德則認為法國債務問題應透過財政政策解決，歐洲央行官員普遍
認為能源價格的潛在波動依然是推升通膨並使其居高不下的主要風險來源。。
⚫ 澳洲央行會議紀錄指出，能源價格上漲與需求韌性可能使通膨更具黏性，保留
進一步升息空間。然而 9 月就業數據意外疲弱，市場大幅下調升息預期，目前
預估 11 月維持利率不變的機率接近 90%。

### Page 3

3  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
美元兌新台幣匯率走勢
Oct/19/2026
＜上週走勢＞
上週台幣匯價高點為 31.890，低點在 32.520，以 32.338 作收，較前一周收盤價
貶值 4 角 6.3 分。
上週台幣走貶，周初，外資延續前周大幅匯出的走勢，雖逢高吸引出口商拋匯，
仍不敵外資匯出力道令台幣走貶；週二台股重挫近千點，外資大幅賣超台股 1653 億
並匯出，台幣接連跌破 32.000、32.100 及 32.200，總成交量 71.3 億，創歷史新高；
周三台股收漲，外資雙向進出，台幣收斂部分跌幅；週四，受美國通膨高於預期影
響，早盤台幣跳空開低，台股跌逾 2%，外資大幅賣超 3025 億，台幣跌破 31.400 關
卡，總成交量 91.2 億，再創歷史新高；週五外資持續匯出，台幣貶至周內低點 32.520，
終場在央行調節之下收在 32.338。
＜未來分析＞
預估本週台幣走勢偏弱。就國際市場而言，美國 CPI、PPI 雙雙高於預期，通

膨壓力仍在，儘管聯準會官員陸續發言表示不急於升息，淡化市場對於聯準會十月
升息預期，惟年底前聯準會仍有升息一碼空間，利率持穩於高位將給予國際美元支
撐；就台灣市場來看，市場對 AI 產業資本支出、融資成本及信用風險產生疑慮，若
賣壓延續，將壓抑市場風險偏好，增加外資調整台股部位及匯出的壓力，令台幣面
臨持續貶值的壓力，惟出口商拋匯將抑制台幣貶值速度，綜合以上，預估本週台幣
走勢偏弱。
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：32.100-32.700

### Page 4

4  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌人民幣匯率走勢  
Oct/19/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
上週人民幣震盪走升。上週人民幣中間價連續走升，週三中間價升至 6.7249 創
三年八個月新高，惟市場靜待美國公布通膨數據，交投謹慎，人民幣僅小幅走升；
週三晚間，美國公布 9 月 CPI 年增率 3.7%、核心 CPI 年增 2.6%，雙雙高於預期，
帶動美元指數升至年內高點 102.945、人民幣貶至周內低點 6.7005；然多位聯準會官
員發言表示聯準會不急於升息，令市場對於聯準會十月升息預期略有降溫，美元指
數自高點回落，激勵人民幣由貶轉升；臨近周末，聯準會主席華許於 IMF 會議發言
並未指引十月聯準會政策利率走向，再度淡化市場對於聯準會十月升息預期，國際
美元走勢偏弱，人民幣走升觸及年內高點 6.6825，終場境外人民幣收在 6.6850，週
線升值 91 點，境內人民幣則收在 6.6870，週線升值 60 點。

＜未來分析＞ 
     預估本週人民幣震盪偏升。就國際市場來看，美國仍面臨高通膨、年底可能將
再次升息，利差因素支撐美元指數於高檔震盪，限縮人民幣升值空間；就中國國內
市場來看，中間價持續偏升將帶動人民幣走升，加以出口商實質拋匯需求將支撐人
民幣表現；最後就技術面來看，USD/CNH 日線圖中，匯價維持在五日均線之下交
投，均線呈現空頭排列，加以 USD/CNH 有效跌破 6.6900 整數關卡，顯示 USD/CNH
空方勢力較盛，綜合以上，預估本週人民幣震盪偏升。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌人民幣：6.6500-6.7000

### Page 5

5  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌日圓匯率走勢  
         Oct/19/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週 USD/JPY 先上後下。週初，受油價攀升推升美國通膨與利率預期帶動，美
元指數溫和走升創 6 月底以來新高，推升 USD/JPY 突破 158.50；週間，美國公布 9
月 CPI 高於預期，帶動美元指數升至 2025 年 5 月以來高點，也使 USD/JPY 最高來
到 158.94，然逢高獲利了結賣壓出籠使匯價回落；臨近週末，USD/JPY 一度下探至
157.53，隨後逢低買盤進場收復部分跌幅，終場收在 157.99，週線下跌 13 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 USD/JPY 區間震盪，主要原因如下：一、受美伊戰事推升汽柴油飆漲
與服務成本擴散影響，美國 9 月 CPI 及 PPI 月增漲幅均超出市場預期，加上初請失
業金人數連五週低於 20 萬人顯示勞動市場穩定，市場預期聯準會將於 12 月再次升
息，美日實質利差維持高檔，持續為 USD/JPY 提供下檔支撐。二、被視為鴿派的日
本經濟財政大臣城內實明確表態，貨幣政策應交由日本央行自行判斷，並宣告日本
已擺脫長期通縮、轉型為增長導向經濟體。此番言論降低政治干預日銀進一步升息
的疑慮，在貨幣政策正常化的腳步下，預計將限縮 USD/JPY 的上行空間。三、就技
術面而言，日線圖中，USD/JPY 匯價目前交投於月線及年線之間，各天期均線高度
糾結，多空雙方於 158 關卡附近拉鋸，在有效突破季線及年線反壓或跌破月線支撐
前，仍以區間震盪看待。綜合以上，預估 USD/JPY 本週呈現區間震盪的可能性較高。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌日圓：157.00-159.00 
前週交易區間 
157.53 158.94

6  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
歐元兌美元匯率走勢  
         Oct/19/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週 EUR/USD 震盪走升。週初，持續受法國財政赤字與歐債利差擔憂拖累，
EUR/USD 震盪走弱；週間，因美國 9 月 CPI 高於預期，市場重新評估聯準會年底前
升息腳步，導致 EUR/USD 跳水最低來到 1.1112；臨近週末，儘管美國 PPI 與零售
銷售數據偏熱，市場優先反映美債殖利率回落與風險資產重新定價，美元多頭獲利
了結帶動 EUR/USD 谷底反彈，最高來到 1.1269，終場收在 1.1239，週線上漲 42 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 EUR/USD 區間偏空震盪，主要原因如下：一、法國央行月度展望報
告指出，法國第三季 GDP 預估僅微幅成長 0.1%，印證近期高能源的成本已實質拖
累歐元區核心成員國增長。同時，歐洲央行總裁拉加德明確拒絕出手干預法債，要
求法國透過自身財政政策化解赤字困境；在明年法國大選前的財政隱憂與基本面疲
弱下，預計將令歐元上檔持續承壓。二、儘管歐洲央行管委溫施與總裁拉加德保留
12 月升息預期，加上德國基建與軍費支出為歐元提供部分下檔保護，但市場穩固預
期聯準會年底前將再升息一次。在美國經濟韌性與高利差優勢主導下，預計將限縮
歐元反彈空間。三、技術面而言，在日線圖中，EUR/USD 自低點反彈並站回五日線
之上，然而中長期均線下彎仍維持空頭排列，RSI 指標亦處於 50 中軸下方，顯示整
體空方趨勢尚未扭轉，反彈至月線附近恐面臨較大賣壓。綜合以上，預估 EUR/USD
本週呈現區間偏空震盪的可能性較高。 
＜預估本週＞ 歐元兌美元：1.1150-1.1300 
前週交易區間 
1.1112 1.1269

7  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
                           新台幣利率走勢分析

115. 10.12-115.10.16 
銀行隔夜拆款利率 0.805% ~ 0.830%  
30 天期短票利率   1.67% ~ 1.68% 
公債－10 年期-A15109     2.020~2.025% 
－ 5 年期- A15108 1.88%

一、 台幣貨幣市場部分，本週央行連續減發定存單使市場資金寬鬆，雖外資大舉匯
出，資金供給仍充裕，帶動短率朝區間低位移動。 
週五央行標售 2 年期定存單 250 億元，加權平均得標利率為 1.58%，低於市場
預期區間 1.60%-1.62%，較上期標售結果下跌 5.8 個基本點。 
週 五 隔 夜 拆 款 利 率 區 間 在 0.805%~0.830% ， 30 天 期 短 票 主 要 成 交 在
1.67%~1.68%，利率大致持平於上週。 
二、 5 年期指標券 A15108 週二搓合成交在 1.88%，後隨美債利率滑落，加上本週
2 年期公債標債結果提振市場持券信心，帶動 10 年期指標券 A15109 週四向
下成交在 2.02%~2.025%。 
在銀行去化短期資金及補充 LCR 之需求下，週三 2 年期公債得標利率為 1.65%，
落在市場預估區間下緣，投標倍數回升至 2.86 倍，銀行業得標比重上升至 88%。
股市承壓帶動避險需求，美債利率大幅回檔，促使台債交投偏空氛圍轉趨和
緩，惟在央行維持緊縮貨幣政策下，買盤積極追價的意願不強，預期短線台債
殖利率呈區間格局的可能性較高，關注下週 10 年券標售結果，預估 5 年券區
間為 1.85%~1.90%，10 年券區間為 1.98%~2.03%。

### Page 1

Summary: Acter held an investor conference on 20 November, after its 3Q26 results 
release on 9 November 2026.

We have a Buy (1) rating on the stock with a 12-month TP of TWD1,415, based on a target 
PER of 20x, applied to our 1-year-forward EPS. For more information on this company, 
please refer to our initiation report, Initiation: acting on the demand for cleanrooms, on 7 
October 2026.

What’s the impact 
• 3Q26 results: margin recovered faster than we expected. Acter’s gross margin came 
in at 24.3% (improved from 20.6% in 2Q26 and 17.8% in 3Q25), above our estimate 
(23.0%) and the consensus (24.1%), thanks to the revenue recognition of large-scale 
cases with costs partially recognised in 2Q26. Thus, the operating margin of 18.9% also 
beat our estimate (17.9%) but was slightly lower than the consensus (19.1%).

• Intact global demand for cleanroom technology, the edge AI market, and rapid 
cleanroom expansion in Asia further support Acter’s order backlog. Its backlog 
reached TWD84bn at the end of 3Q26 vs. TWD46bn in 2025 and TWD30bn in 2022 
(15.3% 2022-25 CAGR), which largely outpaced the global cleanroom technology 
market’s forecast CAGR. The company sees strong clean room demand in light of 
demand from the OSAT, ODM and memory segments but the labour shortage should be 
the bottle neck for the sector. In the near and medium term (up to 2 years), the company 
expects the gross margin to be 22-28%, subject to inflation. However, Acter foresees a 
favourable gross margin trend in the long run (ie. 3-5 years) on rising oversees revenue 
contribution. There should be some upside potential for gross margin from rush orders.

What we recommend 
We reaffirm our Buy (1) rating with a 12-month target price of TWD1,415, based on a 
target PER of 20x, applied to our 1-year forward EPS. Based on our 2026/27 EPS 
estimates, the stock is currently trading at PERs of 11.6x/8.4x, vs. its past-3-year trading 
range of 7-23x (average: 12x). Key downside risks: weaker-than-expected expansion in 
the semiconductor industry; project delays or cancellations.

In the interests of timeliness, this document has not been edited.

• AST2700 transition and pricing. AST2700 represents ASPEED’s most 
important product-cycle driver. Management expects shipments to exceed 
10 million units in 2027 and believes AST2700 could account for more than 
half of its major BMC mix in 2028. Indicative ASP is approximately 50% 
above current AST2600. ASPEED implemented an approximately 10% price 
increase from December 1, 2026, following another increase earlier in the 
year. The latest adjustment is intended to offset higher foundry and other 
supplier costs and applies to products shipped after the effective date. 
Because BMCs represent a very small proportion of total server or rack 
costs, management does not expect the increase to materially affect 
customer purchasing decisions. Market-share gains at hyperscale 
customers and continued migration toward AST2700 should allow blended 
ASP to rise even if unit growth moderates. Supply conditions are expected 
to improve around mid-2027, enabling shipments to align more closely 
with underlying customer demand.
• Product mix and GM outlook. Peripheral controllers have expanded faster 
than management initially expected and may now represent 15–20% of 
demand, compared with the previous 10% assumption, per management. 
Growth is coming from networking switches, cooling systems and other 
infrastructure surrounding compute servers. The new AST1840 solution 
combines ASPEED’s management controller with a Lattice FPGA, adding

programmability, hardware security and a smaller footprint for BMC add-in cards. These higher-function products
should carry ASPs above first-generation devices. The GM outlook depends on product mix and cost pass-through
rather than pricing alone. Management indicated that rising input costs will limit the immediate GM benefit of price
increases, but richer AST2700 and peripheral mix should support longer-term profitability.

• Architecture exposure, market turbulence and risks. BMC content varies by system. Management offered rough
planning ratios of approximately one BMC per GPU in certain NVIDIA configurations, one BMC for every three
accelerators in some ASIC platforms, and one BMC per one or two CPUs in conventional servers. More switches,
cooling subsystems and power-management elements could also increase management-controller content even if
the number of compute racks changes. While there is some concern on a solution for IMC  (Infrastructure

Management Controller), ASPEED believes that rack-level control remains fragmented among dedicated servers,
switches, power-rack controllers and software, thus board-level BMC functions cannot be replaced because they
must operate independently when the host processor or operating system fails. Overall, despite rising investor
concerns on customer inventory accumulation and delayed platform deployments, ASPEED's diversification across
GPU, ASIC, CPU and peripheral applications keeps its growth outlook positive.

3
Aspeed Technology
Valuation
We value Aspeed at NT$20,700 based on a historical mid-cycle multiple (since 2020) of 50x average 2027E-28E EPS, as we
believe it fairly reflects the company’s earnings performance and industry dynamics, secular growth trends, and Aspeed’s
strong supply chain position, as well as robust earnings growth amid margin expansions.
Risks
Our quant rating system rates the Aspeed stock High Risk based on its volatility over the past year, which we think is

unwarranted given the company's dominance and outlook in the BMC sector. Key downside risks to our earnings forecasts and
target price include: 1) weaker-than-expected growth in servers; 2) slower-than-expected CPU platform migration; 3) peer
competition; and 4) limited progress in product diversification.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.

### Page 3

Investment Thesis & PT methodology and risks

Victory Giant is a leading AI PCB supplier, with continued investment in R&D and 
capacity expansion to support long-term growth. We are positive on the company’s 
growth prospects, driven by rising AI infrastructure demand, PCB specification upgrades 
toward higher layer counts and higher-end materials, increasing PCB usage in AI servers 
to replace copper cables, and customer expansion into ASIC servers. We expect 
migration to next-generation AI platforms to support higher dollar content, while 
continued capacity expansion should enable the company to capture growing demand. 
We are Buy-rated on Victory Giant. 
We are Buy-rated on Shennan. The company is a key PCB supplier in China that focuses 
on high-end markets, including, telecom, datacom, automotive, industrials, medicals, 
etc. We see the company as a key beneficiary of China’s domestic AI infrastructure 
investments, and therefore expect a robust revenue and net profit growth trend for 
Shennan as local equipment vendors ramp up their server/networking shipment. Current 
valuation is attractive as it trades at a discount to historical average. 
WUS is a leading global PCB supplier, expanding from telecom equipment, networking 
devices, to high-speed networking in AI data center: 800G / 1.6T switch PCB, switch 
board in rack-level AI servers, midplane / backplane in rack-level AI servers to replace 
copper cables, etc. We are positive on WUS growth ahead, driven by (1) AI infrastructure 
ramp up, (2) PCB specification upgrade along with rising computing density per rack and 
high-speed networking, (3) PCB usage increase per AI servers rack, replacing copper 
cables, easier for assembly, and (4) strong commitment in both R&D and capacity 
expansion, supporting the company’s ability to capture the rising PCB trend. We are 
Buy-rated on WUS. WUS currently trades at 15.8x 2027E P/E and we believe the 
valuation is attractive considering +48% NI YoY on avg. in 2027-28E and OPM up to 26% 
by 2028E from 24% in 9M25.  
Shengyi Tech is a leading copper-clad laminate (CCL) supplier and a key beneficiary of 
growing AI infrastructure demand. We are positive on the company’s long-term growth, 
driven by rising AI server shipments, CCL specification upgrades and continued capacity 
expansion. We expect higher transmission speeds in rack-level AI servers to support 
increasing adoption of M9 and above products, driving product mix improvement, while

strong demand should support AI CCL pricing. We believe AI CCL demand growth can
absorb planned industry capacity additions, supporting continued shipment growth and

Valuation: We derive our 12M TP of Rmb540 on a target P/E multiple of 26.3x applied
to our 2027E EPS. Our target P/E of 26.3x is derived from the correlation between P/E
and EPS growth of Victory Giant’s peers, based on the company’s 2028E EPS YoY

growth.
Key risks: Slower-than-expected AI server shipment ramp up pace;
Slower-than-expected AI server PCB specification upgrade; Fiercer-than-expected
market competition.
Price Target Risks and Methodology - Shennan Circuits
We are Buy rated on Shennan Circuits with a 12-month target price of Rmb511, which is
based on a 39.7x target P/E multiple on our 2027E EPS.
Downside risks: 1) slower-than-expected expansion to AI PCB; 2) fiercer-than-expected

competition, which could lead to ASP erosion and margin pressure; 3) customer
concentration risk; and 4) slower-than-expected server/automotive PCB and IC
substrate growth.
Valuation: We derive our 12M TP of Rmb148 on a target P/E multiple of 23x 2027E EPS.
Our target P/E of 23x is derived from the correlation between P/E and EPS growth of
WUS’s peers, based on the company’s 2027E-28E EPS YoY growth.
Key risks: (1) Slower-than-expected high-end AI server and high-speed switch

migration; (2) Fiercer-than-expected competition in AI PCB market; and (3)
Slower-than-expected new capacity expansion.
Valuation: We derive our 12M TP of Rmb255 on a target P/E multiple of 50.4x 2027E
EPS. Our target P/E of 50.4x is derived from the correlation between P/E and EPS growth
of Shengyi Tech’s peers, based on the company’s 2027E-28E EPS YoY growth.
Key risks: Lower-than-expected AI infrastructure investment, Lower-than-expected
allocation, Change in the direction of technology.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.
Coverage Universe: A list of all stocks in each coverage universe is available by primary analyst, stock and coverage universe at

https://www.gs.com/research/hedge.html.

力旺(3529)：2H26 權利金大幅成長、Security 長期機會大 
建議：維持買進

一、2026 年前三季權利金營收
+13.4%YOY，PUF-based 權利
金+927%YOY 
 2026 前三季權利金營收 21.49 億元，+13.4%YOY。 
成熟製程 NeoBit 佔 24.6%，+8.6%YOY。 
先進製程 NeoFuse 佔 70.7%，+12.3%YOY。 
PUF-based 佔 1.5%，+927%YOY。 
MTP 佔 3.2%，+32.5%YOY。 
權利金成長預計將持續加速，主要受惠於先進製程應用的 ASP 提升、客戶新產
品持續放量、PUF 相關部署的營收貢獻增加，以及高權利金費率應用的產品組
合比重提高。

二、2026 年前三季授權金
+38.2% YOY，PUF - based 
+234% YOY 
 2026 前三季授權金營收 12.51 億元，+38.2%YOY。 
NeoBit 佔 11.9%，-39.3%YOY。 
NeoFuse 佔 36.5%，+35.1%YOY。 
PUF-based 佔 31.7%，+234%YOY。 
MTP 佔 19.9%，+22.9%YOY。 
授權業務預期將維持強勁的成長動能，主要受惠於先進與成熟製程節點、硬體安
全、AI 基礎設施，以及次世代快閃記憶體 Flash 應用等領域。

三、維持買進理由  1) 受惠於先進製程應用的 ASP 提升、客戶新產品持續放量、PUF 相關部署的
營收貢獻增加，以及高權利金費率應用的產品組合比重提高，權利金成長預計將
持續加速。 
2) 授權業務預期將維持強勁的成長動能，主要受惠於先進與成熟製程節點、硬
體安全、AI 基礎設施，以及次世代快閃記憶體 Flash 應用等領域。 
3) PUF-based 已貢獻 2026 年前三季營收 12.6%，+252%YOY。應用面包括
CPU、Storage、光通、FPGA、高速傳輸晶片多面向。Arm Neoverse CSS V3
已將力旺的 PUF 列入核心元件。

公司簡介 
上品為從事氟素樹脂材料相關製品之生產
廠商，主要經營氟素樹脂材料（鐵氟龍）相
關製品，包括氟素原料加工之半成品及成品
生產及銷售，下游應用廣泛，是全球氟素樹
脂加工應用廠商中少數能同時滿足各階段
設備、製造、研發、創新、設計等完整服務
的企業。3Q26 產品營收比重，氟素內襯設
備營收占 69.7% ， 氟 素 內 襯 管 件 類 占
10.2%，氟素應用材料類占 16.5 %，氟素
工程收入占 3.7%。3Q26 公司營收按地區
別區分，台灣占 47.4%，中國占 48.2%，美
國占 0.5%，其他亞洲占 1.6%，歐洲占
2.2%。

主要客戶： 
主要競爭對手：

葉家宏 bill.yeh@sinopac.com 
本次報告：法說會

上品 (4770 TT) 
在手訂單金額提升，持續擴充產能因應

永豐觀點 
上品在手訂單金額提升至 80 億元，隨著先前低毛利訂單陸續消化完畢，產品組合
預期改善，公司規劃於 2027 持續進行產能擴充，後市展望持續正向，研究部維持
正向看待上品獲利表現。 
 投資評價與建議 
投資建議維持買進，主要係因：上品在手訂單金額持續提升至 80 億元，反映台灣
及中國地區接單表現持續暢旺，隨著公司低毛利訂單出貨完畢，產品組合應逐步好
轉，且公司規劃於 2027 持續擴充產能，挹注未來營收動能，研究部預估公司
2026/2027 稅後 EPS 14.55/23.01 元，參考歷史 PER 區間約位於 10-20X，以
2027 年預估 EPS，給予 15X PER 評價，目標價 345 元，投資建議維持買進。

ESG 評析 
上品企業永續評鑑整體為 B+等，於 SASB 產業永續議題與跨產業五大永續面向平
均得分分別為 B+等與 B+等。

### Page 2

2

上品為從事氟素樹脂材料相關製品之生產廠商：公司成立於 1981 年，主要經營氟
素樹脂材料（鐵氟龍）相關製品，包括氟素原料加工之半成品及成品生產及銷售，
下游應用廣泛，是全球氟素樹脂加工應用廠商中少數能同時滿足各階段設備、製
造、研發、創新、設計等完整服務的企業。3Q26 產品營收比重，氟素內襯設備營
收占 69.7%，氟素內襯管件類占 10.2%，氟素應用材料類占 16.5 %，氟素工程收
入占 3.7%；3Q26 公司產品依應用類別區分，半導體占營收 95.1%，化工應用占
比為 3.1%，面板及太陽能應用占 1.4%，其他應用占 0.4%，3Q26 公司營收按地
區別區分，台灣占 47.4%，中國占 48.2%，美國占 0.5%，其他亞洲占 1.6%，歐
洲占 2.2%。 
3Q26 EPS 4.27 元，優於預期：上品 3Q26 營收 16 億元（+22.5% QoQ，+14.4% 
YoY），優於預期，主因上品於 2Q26 擴產，並持續拉升產能利用率，推升營收表
現，毛利率 34.64%（-1.91pts. QoQ，+4.91pts. YoY），低於預期，主因上品出
貨較多低毛利訂單，影響產品組合所致，稅後淨利 34.1 億元（+37.2% QoQ，
+39.9% YoY），優於預期，其中，營業費用低於預期，主因認列預期信用損失金
額降低所致，3Q26 稅後 EPS 4.27 元，優於預期。

### Page 3

3

在手訂單金額持續提升：上品在手訂單金額達 80 億元，較前次發報告時 75 億元
持續提升，其中，約 75%為彰濱廠訂單，約 25%為嘉興廠訂單，預期到年底在手
訂單金額將持續再提升。2026 接單槽車數量達 1,100 台，反映台灣化學品廠因應
晶圓廠大客戶需求而擴充產能，對於上品內襯設備及內襯管件需求同步增溫，而中
國記憶體廠及化學品廠亦持續擴充，下單動能有所增溫。隨著市況於 2026 大幅改
善，彰濱廠及嘉興廠已可挑選毛利率較佳的訂單進行承接，產品組合預期將逐步好
轉，有利其毛利率逐步回升。 
2027 上品計畫持續擴充產能：因應客戶需求強勁，公司於 2Q26 進行擴產，並持
續招工補充人力，拉升產能利用率，目前彰濱廠及嘉興廠已達近滿載水準，公司年
初彰濱廠槽車約當產能約為月產能 80 台，近期已提升至月產能 130 台，預期年底
將達滿載規模月產能 140 台，公司規劃將於 6M27 再擴充至月產能 180 台，嘉興
廠亦規劃提升產能約 30%，以因應客戶強勁需求。而先前承接的低毛利訂單仍有
部分尚未出貨完畢，預期將於 1Q27 出貨完畢，因營收比重持續下降，預期產品組
合應持續改善，有利毛利率及獲利表現回升。

後市展望正向，投資建議維持買進：展望後市，台灣化學品廠及中國記憶體、化學
品廠擴建，推升對上品氟素樹脂產品需求，上品在手訂單金額持續提升，隨著上品
先前承接低毛利訂單陸續出貨完畢，產品組合應逐步改善，推升毛利率及獲利表現
回升。上品規劃於 2027 持續進行產能擴充，挹注未來營收動能，且已可挑選毛利
率較佳的訂單進行承接，後市獲利展望正向。研究部預估公司 2026 營收 56.9 億
元（+20.3% YoY），毛利率 35.1%，稅後淨利 11.6 億元（+41.6% YoY），稅後
EPS 14.55 元，預估 2027 營收 76.8 億元（+35.1% YoY），毛利率 38.31%，稅
後淨利 18.4 億元（+59.2% YoY），稅後 EPS 23.01 元，參考歷史 PER 區間約位
於 10-20X，以 2027 年預估 EPS，給予 15X PER 評價，目標價 345 元，維持買進
投資建議。

Document 205: 6515穎崴 亞東

Source PDF: all report pdf/6515穎崴 亞東.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-19
Pages: 7
Status: ok

### Page 1

◼ 投資建議: 
美系 AI GPU 與客製化 ASIC 持續開案，帶動高階測試座需求。模型預估，穎崴的 NVIDIA 
系統級測試座（SLT）營收將由 2026 年的 33.26 億元增至 2027 年的 83.45 億元，貢獻公
司逾半營收增量。公司目標於 2027 上半年將自製彈簧針月產能提升至 1,400 萬針，有助降
低外購成本，支撐全年毛利率由 40.92% 升至 42.97%。穎崴的矽光子與 CPO 測試方案已涵蓋
晶圓、封裝及模組，若客戶專案進入量產，可望帶來額外營收。 
根據模型，穎崴(6515)目標價 5,711 元。依模型預估的 2027 年 EPS 163.18 元 × 基準本益比 
35 倍估算，較 2026/11/18 收盤價 3,560 元具 60.4% 潛在上漲空間。建議買進 
◼ 未來觀察： 
1. 探針自製率&產能狀況 
2. HPC/AI 業務成長性 
◼ 公司是否涉及經營權爭奪之情事：無。 
◼ 產業概況： 
1. 半導體測試介面市場:系統測試需求高速成長。 
AI GPU、伺服器 CPU 與雲端業者自研 ASIC 擴張，帶動高階晶片封裝後最終測試（FT）與
系統級測試（SLT）需求成長。FT 把關量產電性品質；SLT 模擬實際系統與軟體運作，補足 
FT 對系統整合、長時間與極端負載之測試不足，隨晶片複雜度提升，滲透率持續走高。 
市場預估全球高階 HPC FT/SLT Socket TAM 將自 2026E 9 億美元躍升至 2028E 37 億美元，
CAGR 約 104%（SLT 達約 110%）。動能來自時間與耗材乘數效應：2025–2028E AI 晶片 
FT/SLT 測試時間 CAGR 達 15–20%（如 Rubin SLT 增至 120–150+ 分鐘），壓低單槽產出
並迫使客戶擴增平行測試槽位。隨大封裝、萬級以上高 Pin 數、高頻高速及破千瓦功耗成為
主流，高階測試座須整合訊號完整性、大電流與溫控散熱，帶動 FT Socket 單顆價值量提升 2 
至 3 倍；長時高功耗壓接亦加速探針磨損與更換，創造 4 至 5 倍耗材數量乘數效應。

### Page 2

◼ 公司簡介： 
1. 穎崴主要專注於半導體測試介面之研發設計與製造銷售，目前作為全球最大測試座
（Test Socket）供應商。主要產品涵蓋後端 FT Test、SLT Test、與 Burn-in Test 用之同軸
測試座（Coaxial Socket）、 HyperSocket、Burn-in Socket。今年與 TPI 簽訂五年合作契約
積極擴展前端晶圓測試 CP Test 用之 MEMS Probe Card，並整合主動溫控與液冷技術開
發高工耗測試逤需要的散熱測試解決方案。穎崴目前主要生產基地分布於高雄，分別為
高雄探針廠、高雄仁武廠，仁武廠包含一廠與二廠，其中仁武二廠已於 2Q27 投產。新
竹廠負責探針卡製造。 
2. 1H26 營收結構為 Coaxial Socket 48%、Probe Card 27%、PoP & Plastic Socket 14%、
Contact Element 6%、Others 3%、Burn-in Socket 2%。若按應用別區分，HPC & AI 占比
已達 70%居應用類別首位，其次 Smart Phone 8%、而 Networking／車用及其他合計 
22%。至 3Q26，依 Citi 調研，隨探針卡專案出貨階段性收尾及高階測試座放量，FT 
Test、SLT Test & Burn-in Socket 合計比重回升至 72%。其中 Coaxial Socket 預估 60%、
RF & Plastic Socket 大概 11%、Burn-in Socket 則佔 1%。至於 Probe Card 預估降至 20% 
3. 公司整體合併毛利率於 1Q26 為 43.0%、2Q26 因 MEMS 探針卡占比拉升及新產能折舊影
響降至 38.3%，3Q26 隨測試座比重提高毛利率已回升至 40.5%。各產品線方面，依摩
根大通調研，Socket 與 Contact Elements 毛利率穩定維持在 40%+。而 MEMS Probe Card 
因須外購關鍵探針元件，因此即便 2026 年生效之二期協議已改善獲利，毛利率仍僅約 
30–35%。目前穎崴 Pogo pin 自製程度高達 60%，隨自製彈簧針 pogo pin 月產能由 1H26
的 600 萬 pins 擴至 1H27 的 1,400 萬 pins，將有助進一步支撐 Socket 毛利率。 
4. 客戶比重:截至 2026 年活躍客戶超過 200 家，但營收高度集中於高階 AI、HPC、GPU、
ASIC 與伺服器 CPU 客戶。前十大客戶合計占營收比重達 90%，且 7 奈米及以下先進

### Page 3

製程產品占比高達 89%。根據摩根大通，2026 全年穎崴核心終端客戶營收占比以
Nvidia 居首，佔比大約 50–60%。其次為 AMD，預計 10–15%與 Google 約 2%。
◼ 成長動能：
1. AI GPU／ASIC 推升 SLT 與高階 FT 測試座需求)
AI 晶片朝大封裝、高 Pin 數、高功耗與高速傳輸升級，拉長測試時間並放大 SLT Test 導入
需求，使整體市場進入倍數擴張期。在 TAM 動能上，全球高階 HPC FT Test／SLT Test 測試
座市場規模預計自2026E 的 9 億美元躍升至2028E 的 37 億美元，市場預估從 2026 到 2028
相關產品的市場CAGR 將逾 100%，其中價值量更高的 SLT Test 測試座將由 7 億美元跳升至
31 億美元。隨穎崴切入 Nvidia SLT Test 供應鏈，目前市占率預估由 2026E 的 20% 升至
2028E 的30%，外加 2027 年雲端業者如 Google、AMD 的自研 ASIC 與伺服器 CPU 擴大量產，

FT Test／SLT Test 測試座占穎崴總營收比重預估將於 2027E–2028E 維持於 64%–79% 區
間。根據自有模型估算，測試座業務於 2027E 預計貢獻營收約新台幣 161 億–192 億元、
2028E 擴增至約 274 億–362 億元，整體測試座 2026–2028E 營收 CAGR 區間約為 61%–
89%。
2. HyperSocket 與液冷熱管理產品放量
下一代 AI 晶片正由約 10000 Pin 邁向20000 Pin 以上，且由於 AMD MI455、TPUv9 系列新
世代平台封裝尺寸將跨過 100×100mm 至 120×120mm 門檻，基板翹曲與大電流散熱成為測試瓶
頸。穎崴專利 HyperSocket 結合主動液冷熱管理方案可克服大尺寸封裝翹曲並維持穩定電
性，因此Pogo Pin 單針 ASP 相較既有高階同軸測試座享有約 30%溢價，帶動產品組合持續升
級，目前穎崴公司預估 HyperSocket 與液冷測試解決方案將於 2027 占總營收 5%，但考量客

戶大封裝世代切換節奏，普遍預期放量重心將落在 2028 年。依自有模型的預估，該測試解決
方案預計於2027 貢獻總營收約 3%–5%（約新台幣 9 億–15 億元），2028 有機會放大至約
5%–6%（約新台幣 21 億–32 億元），相較 2027 年呈現倍數級年增動能，並有助高階測試座
在擴產自製化下將 2027E–2028E 合併毛利率支撐於 41.1%–46.2% 區間。

### Page 4

3. 新增產能釋放與 CPO 測試
因應既有產能滿載，高雄仁武二廠已於2Q27 投產，其中新增 Socket 產能自 3 月開出並在第
二季順利認列營收，使 5,000-pin 的等效規格 Socket 月產能自 2026 下半年的 4.2k 套擴增逾
70%至 1H27 的 7.2k 套，自製 Pogo Pin 月產能亦由 900 萬 pins 增逾 50%至 1,400 萬 pins，預
估 2027 年底可達單月 1,600–1,800 萬 pins。新產能開出讓既有高階 AI 積壓的訂單順利轉
化為營收，支撐目前市場與自有模型所預估的穎崴 2027E 總營收區間達新台幣 213.5 億–
303.7 億元（YoY +45%–85%）、2028E 達 351.0 億–528.3 億元（YoY +64%–76%），對應
2026–2028E 總營收 CAGR 區間為 54%–81%（EPS CAGR 區間則達 76%–108%）。CPO 的測試
方案預計2H27 開始小量出貨，營收貢獻大約低個位數以下，至 2028 年進入量產成熟階段，

預計可以貢獻2028 年營收最高至接近 10%。
4. 1H26
1H26 合併營收 65.03 億元，HoH +61.0%、YoY +70.3%（1Q26 為 29.80 億元、2Q26 為
35.23 億元），整體毛利率為 40.5%（1Q26 為 43.0%、2Q26 為 38.3%）。稅後淨利 13.71 億
元（YoY +67.6%、HoH +60.4%；1Q26 為 6.99 億元、2Q26 為 6.72 億元），1H26 累計 EPS
達 38.24 元（1Q26 為 19.54 元、2Q26 為 18.70 元），上半年營收與獲利均創歷年同期新
高。截至最新已公告進度，累計前 9 月合併營收達 110.59 億元（年增 96.7%），同創歷年
同期新高。在產品組合與毛利率比較上，1H26 產品營收比重仍以 Coaxial Socket（48%）與
Probe Card（27%）為核心。
⚫ 與1H25（毛利率 48.9%）比較：1H26 毛利率表現明顯較差（YoY -8.4 個百分點），主

因1H25 銷售較多高毛利的 Non-MEMS Probe Card 及傳統高毛利組合，而 1H26 向
Technoprobe 外購關鍵 MEMS 探針元件的 MEMS Probe Card 營收占比擴大，上述外購元
件結構拉低 MEMS Probe Card 毛利率，使其落在 30%–35%，低於測試座及接觸元件的
40% 以上水準，對整體毛利率形成結構性稀釋。

### Page 5

⚫ 2H26E 穎崴合併營收 79.56 億元（HoH +22.3%、YoY +97.0%），毛利率 41.29%（HoH 
+0.82 ppt、YoY -0.58 ppt），歸屬母公司稅後淨利 17.71 億元（HoH +29.2%、YoY 
+107.1%），基本 EPS 48.76 元（HoH +27.5%、YoY +103.5%）。

⚫ 2026E 穎崴合併營收 144.60 億元（YoY +84.0%），毛利率 40.92%（YoY -4.34 
ppt），歸屬母公司稅後淨利 31.42 億元（YoY +87.8%），基本 EPS 87.04 元（YoY 
+85.5%）。 
5. 2026/2027 
產能與資本支出方面，仁武二廠與下一座新廠預計於 2Q27–1Q28 陸續投產，推升 2027 年探
針月產能達 1,400 萬–1,800 萬支、自製率提高至 50%–60%，5,000-pin 等效測試座月產能於 
1H27 達 7.2k 套等於穎崴整體測試座產能將在 2027 倍增，2027–2028 年度預估的資本支出介
於新台幣 20.0 億–24.0 億元，其中自有模型預估 2027 為 20.0 億到 22.0 億元之間、2028 預估
為 22.0 億到 24.0 億元之間。新產品線方面，受惠高階 Functional Burn-in 升級，2027–2028E 
Burn-in 測試座營收預估為新台幣 6.40 億–10.53 億元，2027 模型預估約 6.40 億元、2028 則
有望到 10.53 億元，占總營收約 3%成為未來關注的成長動能之一。CPO 與矽光子測試預計自 
2H27 起小量出貨，2028E 營收貢獻區間為 5%-10%不等的年營收，端看實際量產與交貨情況。。

⚫ 2027E 穎崴合併營收 238.42 億元（YoY +64.9%），毛利率 42.97%（YoY +2.05 ppt），
歸屬母公司稅後淨利 59.53 億元（YoY +89.5%），基本 EPS 163.18 元（ YoY +87.5%）。

⚫ 2028E 穎崴合併營收 407.33 億元（YoY +70.8%），毛利率 44.33%（YoY +1.36 ppt），
歸屬母公司稅後淨利 111.08 億元（YoY +86.6%），基本 EPS 304.52 元（ YoY +86.6%）。

기아 (000270/KS) 
라인업 확대의 성과와 주주환원에 주목 
2027 년 영업이익 7.96 조 원 전망 
2027 년 실적은 매출액 124.3 조 원(YoY +1.0%), 영업이익 7.96 조 원(YoY -
5.3%, OPM 6.4%), 지배주주순이익 6.97 조 원(YoY -0.2%)으로 전망한다. 우호적
이었던 환율 효과가 약해지는 가운데, 높아진 원재료 투입가격과 유럽을 중심으로 한 
판매 경쟁도 수익성에 부담으로 남는다. 판매를 지키기 위한 인센티브 확대까지 감안하
면 물량 증가가 그대로 이익 증가로 이어지기는 어렵다. 다만 이미 투입한 신차의 연간 
판매 기여와 미국, 유럽의 라인업 확대가 비용 부담을 일부 상쇄하면서, 하반기에는 전
년 동기 대비 증익으로 돌아설 것으로 본다.

지역별로 갖춘 제품, PBV 로 넓어지는 고객 
2027 년에는 이미 출시한 신차의 판매를 늘려 제품과 설비에 투자한 성과를 매출과 이
익으로 연결하는 일이 중요하다. 미국에서는 텔루라이드 등 주력 SUV 와 확대된 하이
브리드 차종으로 수요에 대응한다. 전기차 전환을 서두르지 않는 고객에게도 선택지를 
제공하며, 이 가운데 현지 생산 차종은 완성차 수입에 따른 관세 부담을 줄이는 데도 도
움이 된다. 유럽에서는 EV2·EV4 등 전기차와 신형 셀토스로 판매 기회를 넓힌다. 기
존 슬로바키아 공장을 활용해 전기차를 공급할 수 있지만, 중국 업체와의 경쟁 속에서 
판촉 부담을 관리하는 과제도 남아 있다.

PBV 는 PV5 에 이어 PV7 을 투입하며 물류 및 운송업체의 용도별 수요에 대응할 계
획이다. 차량 크기와 적재 수요에 맞춘 제품에 판매와 정비망을 더하면 고객과의 거래
를 넓힐 수 있다. 미국 하이브리드의 판매 확대, 유럽 전기차의 안착, PBV 의 고객 확보
는 각각 이익에 기여하는 속도가 다르다. 따라서 판매 증가와 함께 제품 구성의 변화, 
신규 차종의 초기 비용이 수익성에 미치는 영향을 살펴야 한다.

투자의견 매수, 목표주가 12 만 원으로 커버리지 개시 
투자의견 매수, 목표주가 12 만 원으로 커버리지를 개시한다. 당사 12 개월 선행 EPS 에 
최근 1 년 평균 P/E 6.5 배를 적용했다. 연간 감익 부담은 남아 있지만, 지역별 수요에 
대응할 제품과 생산기반은 확대돼 있다. 낮아진 밸류에이션 부담을 감안하면, 이미 갖
춘 라인업의 판매 성과와 총주주환원율 35% 이상의 정책에 주목해야 한다는 판단이
다. 
2026-11-25

모빌리티/피지컬AI. 권순우 / soonwoo@sks.co.kr / 02-3773-9592

본 보고서에 언급된 종목의 경우 당사 조사분석담당자는 본인의 담당종목을 보유하고 있지 않습니다.

한화에어로스페이스 (012450) 
3Q26 Review: 기대치 하회. 이제는 용기를 낼 시점

▶ Analyst 채운샘 unsam1@hanwha.com 3772-8419

주가 추이

3분기 영업이익은 지상방산 매출 감소와 일회성 비용 등의 영향으로 
기대치를 대폭 하회했습니다. 4분기 실적 회복과 중장기 실적 성장 대
비 저평가된 밸류에이션에 주목합니다. 
3Q26 Review: 영업이익 0.5조원으로 컨센서스 대폭 하회 
3분기 매출액은 6.1조원으로 전년대비 6.0% 감소했다. 항공우주 부문
과 연결자회사들 성장에도 불구하고 지상방산 매출이 전년대비 약 0.9
조원 감소한 영향이다. 지상방산 내수와 수출 매출은 각각 0.7조원, 0.6
조원으로 전년대비 28.6%, 50.4% 감소했다. 내수는 대전 사업장 생산 
중단, 수출은 4분기 납기 집중에 기인한다. 항공우주 매출은 군수사업 
물량 증가로 전년대비 9.3% 증가한 0.7조원을 기록했다. 연결 자회사
인 한화오션과 한화시스템 매출은 각각 3.4조원, 0.8조원으로 전년대비 
12.9%, 30.1% 증가했다. 영업이익은 0.5조원으로 전년대비 49.2% 감
소했으며 영업이익률은 7.4%로 6.3%P 하락했다. 한화오션과 한화시스
템의 이익 증가에도 지상방산 이익 감소와 필리조선소 적자 반영이 영
향을 미쳤다. 지상방산은 일회성 비용 약 460억원, 원화 강세, 매출 감
소에 따른 고정비 부담 증가가 복합적으로 작용했다. 세전이익은 한화
오션의 외화환산손실 영향으로 -0.2조원을 기록하며 적자전환했다.  
실적 회복과 저평가 매력에 주목할 시점 
4분기 영업이익은 1.7조원으로 추정하며 시장 기대치를 상회할 전망이
다. 지상방산 수출 인도 물량이 집중되는 영향이다. 2027년 예상 실적 
기준 P/E는 12.5배로 경쟁사 Rheinmetall(22.5배) 대비 낮다. 약 35조
원의 방산 수주잔고를 기반으로 최소 2029년까지 실적 성장이 예상되
는 만큼 저평가 매력이 높다. 신규 수주 시점과 실적에 대한 우려가 상
존하지만 현재 밸류에이션에는 상당 부분 반영된 것으로 판단한다. 미
국 중간선거 등 국방예산 관련 정책 불확실성과 환율 부담이 완화되면 
투자자들의 관심은 4분기 실적과 2027년 신규 수주로 이동할 전망이
다. 연말 주요국의 국방예산 확정은 신규 수주 시점에 대한 불확실성을 
낮추는 계기가 될 수 있다.  
투자의견 BUY, 목표주가 1,300,000원 유지 
글로벌 재무장 수요 확대에 따른 동사의 중장기 성장 전망은 여전히 
유효하다. 투자의견 BUY와 목표주가 1,300,000원을 유지한다.

이 자료는 조사분석 담당자가 객관적 사실에 근거해 작성하였으며, 타인의 부당한 압력이나 간섭없이 본인의 의견을 정확하게 
반영했습니다. 본인은 이 자료에서 다룬 종목과 관련해 공표일 현재 관련 법규상 알려야 할 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 본인은 이 
자료를 기관투자자 또는 제 3 자에게 사전에 제공한 사실이 없습니다. (채운샘) 
저희 회사는 공표일 현재 이 자료에서 다룬 종목의 발행주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
한화에어로스페이스는 당사와 ‘독점규제 및 공정거래에 관한 법률’ 에 따른 계열회사 관계에 있는 법인입니다. 
이 자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위해 당사 고객에 한하여 배포되는 자료로서 저작권이 당사에 있으며 불법 복제 및 배포를 금
합니다. 이 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료나 정보출처로부터 얻은 것이지만, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완
전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 이 자료는 어떠한 경우에도 고객의 증권투자 결과와 관련된 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙으로 사
용될 수 없습니다.

[ 종목 투자등급 ] 
당사는 개별 종목에 대해 향후 1 년간 +15% 이상의 절대수익률이 기대되는 종목에 대해 Buy(매수) 의견을 제시합니다. 또한 절대수
익률 -15~+15%가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Hold(보유) 의견을, -15% 이하가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Sell(매도) 의견을 제시합니다. 밸류
에이션 방법 등 절대수익률 산정은 개별 종목을 커버하는 애널리스트의 추정에 따르며, 목표주가 산정이나 투자의견 변경 주기는 종
목별로 다릅니다.

[ 산업 투자의견 ] 
당사는 산업에 대해 향후 1 년간 해당 업종의 수익률이 과거 수익률에 비해 양호한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Positive(긍정
적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 또한 향후 1 년간 수익률이 과거 수익률과 유사한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Neutral(중립적) 
의견을, 과거 수익률보다 부진한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Negative(부정적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 산업별 수익률 전
망은 해당 산업 내 분석대상 종목들에 대한 담당 애널리스트의 분석과 판단에 따릅니다.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC INSIGHT
• Unemployment and output both show a common pattern
of downturns being short, sharp and irregular, while the
subsequent recoveries and expansions are longer and
slower. The eminent economist Milton Friedman likened
this pattern to plucking on a string. The causes are unclear,

though there are some plausible candidates.
• If we take the “plucking” pattern seriously, there are clear
policy implications. Standard models used by central banks
do not allow for this pattern and therefore systematically
overstate how often capacity is tight. And when a
downturn does occur, the “plucking” view argues for
forceful stabilisation policy.
• We believe the current slow upward grind in
unemployment in Australia is a different phenomenon

and expect slow growth rather than a sharp downturn. If
people start thinking current inflation pressures demand a
1980s-style “Volcker disinflation”, though, it could just end
up plucking the economy.
Look at a graph of the unemployment rate for almost any
western economy, and you will notice a few things. The
big increases are sharp and sudden, while the subsequent
recoveries are slow and last longer. The timing of those upward
spikes is irregular, with no obvious pattern. The height of the

spike in unemployment is a good predictor of how far the rate
declines in the recovery that follows. By contrast, neither the
length of an expansion phase of declining unemployment, nor
the size of the decline predict the size of the next downturn.
This pattern is a far cry from the notion of a business
cycle, where output and unemployment oscillate round
some average or trend. There is something different about
downturns. They are not just like expansions but a bit slower.

The eminent economist Milton Friedman described this as a
“plucking” model. The economy, he suggested, was a bit like a
guitar string. Most of the time it stayed close to the fretboard.
There is a physical maximum amount of production possible
at any one time. Occasionally, though, the economy could be
plucked away from that maximum, with output falling and
unemployment rising sharply.
Remarkably, the mainstream macroeconomic literature that
central banks rely on has engaged little with this feature of the

data. Forecasting models used by the RBA and its peers do not
produce this pattern. Some authors have looked at “plucking”-
type models, though. Their analysis confirmed that downturns
predict the size of the following expansion, but the reverse
Is the economy ‘plucked’?
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
is not true. Expansions do not “die of old age” by building up
imbalances. Australia went for 30 years without a proper
downturn: it turns out that this is entirely possible provided

nothing bad enough happens.
These authors further argue that the maximum production level
during expansions is buffeted by small shocks, some temporary,
others positive and permanent – think new technologies and
ways of doing things, or a policy change that encourages
workforce participation. Downturns, by contrast, are triggered
by large shocks that are usually temporary and arrive at irregular
intervals. Some argue that it is best to think of economies as

having three phases: normal expansions, which can continue
for long periods; shorter-lived downturns; and subsequent
recovery phases where growth can outstrip normal rates and
unemployment declines, but at a slower pace than it rose.
Some downturns do not end in full recovery, however. This
observation links to another strand of research showing that
the output losses associated with financial crises and wars are
not fully recovered. The experience after the Global Financial

Crisis shows this pattern in a number of advanced economies.
Standard models would interpret this as slower growth in
potential output, perhaps feeding the Australian tradition of
hand-wringing about insufficient productivity or reform.
While the pattern in the data is clear, the causes of “plucking”
are far from settled, with some authors seeing boom–bust
dynamics as inherent in the economic system rather than
driven by shocks. Some recent papers – including preliminary

RBA work – look to certain frictions in the labour market
for an explanation. These include search frictions, which
arise because workers and firms sometimes require time to
find a good match with each other. Another at least partial
explanation is that workers resist absolute wage cuts but
accept falls in purchasing power when dollar wages are
constant in the face of positive inflation.
An objection to this line of research is the finding by some BIS

researchers that the plucking pattern is clearer in countries
with more flexible labour markets – mostly English-speaking
ones. The literature focusing on labour market frictions also
does not explain where the originating pluck comes from,
“Downturns are usually short, sharp and
arrive at irregular intervals; expansions are
longer and slower."

### Page 3

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
3
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
only why the data have the dynamics they do when those
shocks occur. Something must be causing the “pluck” shock,
if indeed it is a shock. The BIS paper suggests one possibility:
the authors find that output in the manufacturing and trade
sectors shows a clearer pluck pattern than construction.

Construction downturns tend to linger, perhaps because of
their connections to indebtedness and financial crises.
If we take seriously the patterns in the data showing
asymmetric downturns and the plucking pattern Friedman
described, there are clear policy implications. Currently, the
models underlying central bank forecasts are symmetric by
construction. They therefore systematically understate spare
capacity during expansions. They will find a positive output

gap ahead of a downturn, even when there was no evidence of
overheating at the time. This oversight risks policy mistakes.
The asymmetric nature of downturns also means that the
benefits of macroeconomic stabilisation policy are mostly
about cushioning downturns and hastening recoveries. In
normal times, the benefits will be small.
We also note that the RBA paper mentioned above hypothesised
a short-run NAIRU and a long-run NAIRU that are both pushed

around by actual unemployment in a path-dependent pattern.
This is an argument for “holding on to the employment gains”
that may have been missed by people whose mental model of
the economy is fully symmetric, without any plucking.
The plucking model started from empirical observations
about large downturns. The kind of slow grind upwards in
unemployment seen in Australia in the 2010s and in the past
couple of years does not fit this pattern. It is a qualitatively

different phenomenon requiring other explanations, including
perhaps the path-dependent drag upwards of the feasible
unemployment rate by the actual unemployment rate.
It is these kinds of considerations that underlie our current
forecast for slow growth and not a downturn, even though this
week’s labour market data were concerning. A further 25 basis
points or so of rate increases is unlikely to be the thing that
causes a severe downturn. Typically, downturns are not just

slower expansions but are qualitatively different phases that
require a trigger.
The one thing that makes me nervous is that the plucking
literature finds that every downturn is different. The famous
opening line to Tolstoy’s Anna Karenina, “Happy families are
all alike; every unhappy family is unhappy in its own way,”
would seem to apply to economies as well. And in at least one
case, a forceful tightening of monetary policy was the trigger
for a downturn. The so-called “Volcker disinflation” brought

US inflation under control in the early 1980s. That episode is
widely regarded as a success, despite the output costs. In the
current circumstances, some policymakers and commentators
might be tempted to believe that the same medicine is needed;
nobody wants inflation expectations to become entrenched,
after all. However, as RBA Board member Ian Ross has noted,
the institutions of the 1970s that produced wage–price spirals
are no longer operative. So there is no guarantee that a similar

approach nowadays would be necessary or as effective.
Watch out then, if the discourse turns to arguments that
countries “need a recession” to get inflation under control. A
Volcker-style policy could just end up plucking the economy.
Forecast changes
• We have upgraded our WPI forecast following this week's data
release, with our year-end forecast now 3.4%yr.
• We have also upgraded our near-term unemployment rate,
with a year-end forecast of 4.9%.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: risks mount
risks are skewed to the upside. FOMC members who spoke
throughout the week also kept the focus on price risks, and called
for the FOMC to act swiftly. Governor Waller again proposed a
50bp hike in December; others seemingly favour a 25bp hike at

the December and January meetings, as Westpac forecasts, and
continuing to tighten further in 2027 if necessary.
Very clearly, the labour market and activity growth are not
of concern for US policy makers. Retail sales gave further
justification for this view this week, the control group
surprising to the upside with a 0.6% gain in October after
a material upward revision to September (0.5%). That said,
housing is under considerable pressure, pending home sales

falling 17.1% over the past year, and housing starts back at
2019 levels despite a 9.9% rebound in October. Persistent
weakness in this sector will, in time, have implications for both
consumption and investment.
China’s October data round was the other key international
development of the week. Again, it provided a call for pro-
active stimulus in scale. In the month, annual retail sales
growth almost stalled at 0.4%yr and the decline in fixed
asset investment broadened outside property, with capital

expenditure now down 8.1% across the economy year-to-
date. Industrial production again highlighted that its output to
satiate global demand which is holding up aggregate growth;
though, even here, momentum softened in October, annual
growth in production slowing from 5.5%yr to 4.8%yr. It is not
only activity that remains under pressure, but also wealth,
with new / existing home prices 0.15% / 0.24% lower in the
month. As we continue to emphasise, to prove effective,

stimulus needs to affect change in both activity and wealth
and do so in a sustainable manner.
Australia’s labour market disappointed again in October,
employment falling 43.5k following September’s 36.4k
decline. On a three-month average basis, job gains over the
year have now slowed to 1.1%yr, materially below current
growth in the working-age population, 1.7%yr. As a result, the
unemployment rate rose to a new post-pandemic high of 4.9%
despite the participation rate falling another 0.2ppts to 66.6%.

While employment growth’s descent initially reflected softer
momentum in hiring, now we are seeing evidence of a material
increase in redundancies. Most affected workers look to have
remained in employment to date, but persistent job shedding
raises the risk of a faster and more persistent uptrend in the
unemployment rate, potentially above our baseline peak
forecast of 5.2% (at mid-2027) and most certainly before the
late-2028 peak of 5.1% the RBA expects.

As such, the robust 1.1% (3.5%yr) rise in the Q3 Wage Price
Index warrants close scrutiny. The Q3 increase was skewed
towards those on awards and enterprise arrangements – pay
outcomes directly tied to the Fair Work Commission’s decision
and collective bargaining. Individual arrangements meanwhile,
the most responsive to current and expected labour market
conditions, accounted for their smallest share of a Q3 wage
increase since 2019. Business survey detail also suggests labour

cost growth will normalise quickly after the award increase.
Deteriorating economic conditions are also increasingly evident
in housing. The Westpac Housing Sentiment Index fell 2.2%
to 83.2 in November, among the weakest readings since 2005.
Interestingly, there remains considerable latent demand: almost
13% of consumers intend to purchase a home over the next
year, and relatively few plan to sell. For now, though, weak
sentiment, high interest rates and expectations of further price

declines are keeping would-be buyers on the sidelines. These
factors also pose risks to the broader growth story.
In the US, abstracting from the Anthropic IPO, the outlook for
inflation remained the critical theme. The October FOMC minutes
highlighted that inflation is currently being supported by tariffs
and energy prices as well as the AI buildout, with "several
[members] judg[ing] that price pressures were increasingly
extending beyond industries directly affected by data center

construction”. Participants "assessed that medium- and longer-
term inflation expectations continued to be consistent with the
Committee's 2 percent objective", although persistent above-
target inflation is a threat to the status quo, and perceived
Elliot Clarke, Head of International Economics
Ryan Wells, Economist
Illiana Jain, Economist
“Australia’s labour market delivered a second
consecutive shock in October, pushing annual
employment growth well below working-age

population growth."

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FOCUS ON NEW ZEALAND
Sunny side up
This week we published our final Economic Overview
for 2026. Titled “Sunny side up”, the report shows that
New Zealand’s economic recovery is continuing. Activity
expanded by 2.6% in the year to June, and recent indicators

point to further moderate growth. Overall, GDP is forecast
to grow by 2.8% in both 2027 and 2028. But this remains a
two-speed recovery: export-facing industries and regional
centres are enjoying stronger incomes, while households
and businesses tied to the major metropolitan centres are
still contending with high inflation, a soft labour market and
a subdued housing market.
The global backdrop remains broadly supportive. On an export-
weighted basis, New Zealand’s trading partners are forecast

to grow by 3.1% in 2026, 2.9% in 2027 and 2.8% in 2028.
Stronger growth in the United States and East Asia outside
China, helped by the continuing artificial intelligence investment
buildout, is offsetting some of the drag from higher energy
prices. The central assumption underpinning these forecasts is
that a Middle East agreement eventually restores safe passage
through the Strait of Hormuz, allowing Brent crude to fall
towards US$77 a barrel in late 2027 and US$71 in late 2028.

Against that backdrop, the external sector is the clear bright
spot. Merchandise export volumes are estimated to have risen
by more than 4% this year, with dairy, meat and horticulture
joined by stronger output of manufactured metals, machinery
and equipment. Favourable growing conditions, high world
prices and a low New Zealand dollar have driven this growth.
Services exports are performing even more strongly and
are on track to grow by around 9% this year. Foreign visitor

arrivals continue to recover strongly, with Australians and
Chinese accounting for about two-thirds of the increase
seen this year. In 12-month rolling terms, visitor numbers
are expected to soon move above their 2019 peak, while
other services exports will likely continue their longer-run
outperformance.
Agriculture is expected to remain a major source of strength.
Farmgate returns are expected to stay elevated despite high
energy and shipping costs and an expected modest appreciation

in the New Zealand dollar. Meat export prices should remain
high in real terms over the next couple of years, with beef
supported by firm US demand. Kiwifruit and apple prices are
also expected to remain strong, while log prices should be
broadly stable to slightly firmer in 2027. Dairy prices – at least
powders – appear to have received an additional lift from El
Niño-related drought risk and strong global demand for protein.
We have raised our forecast milk price for the current season

by 50 cents to $10.25 per kilogram of milk solids – still ahead
of Fonterra’s own forecast, which was revised up to a $10.10
midpoint this week. We have also lifted our 2027/28 season
forecast to $9.75, reflecting a more gradual expected rise in the
New Zealand dollar than foreseen previously.
At this point, the strength offshore has not yet translated
into an equally convincing recovery for households. Average
household earnings grew only 2.9% in the year to June, while

high inflation pushed real wage growth into negative territory.
Real per capita household spending was flat over the year, and
weak house prices have limited growth in household wealth.
Spending should improve gradually during 2027 as export
income circulates through the economy, employment recovers
and inflation eases. Even so, the strongest gains are likely to
remain in regions directly exposed to agriculture and tourism.
The labour market has been unusually slow to respond to

improving GDP. Unemployment has climbed to an 11-year high
of 5.7%, and employment growth has failed to keep pace with
population growth. The pattern is best described as “low-
fire, low-hire”: firms avoided the scale of layoffs that might
normally accompany a downturn but may now have enough
staff to accommodate the early stages of a recovery without
immediately stepping up recruitment. Employment appears
to have begun turning higher and hiring intentions have

recovered, but unemployment is unlikely to decline materially
until next year. We expect it to ease to 5.1% by the end of
2027 and 4.5% in 2028.
Housing remains a restraint. National house prices have fallen
about 1.7% this year, sales have weakened and properties are
taking longer to sell. Adjusted for inflation, house prices are on
course for a fourth consecutive annual decline, and we expect
them to fall by around 33% between the 2021 peak and the

end of 2027. That adjustment is improving affordability and
supporting first-home buyers, but it also encourages existing
owners to save rather than spend. As with other economic
indicators, regional differences remain pronounced, with areas
benefiting from agricultural and tourism income outperforming
the major centres.
Residential construction should nevertheless rise during
2027, supported by a pipeline of more than 40,000 consented
homes, much of it medium-density development in Auckland

and Canterbury. The durability of that upswing is uncertain,
however, because financing costs, building costs and weak
house prices remain significant obstacles. Commercial
construction is closer to finding a floor, although office and
retail development is likely to stay subdued for a while given
elevated vacancy rates.
Darren Gibbs
Senior Economist

### Page 6

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
6
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
Business investment has been more resilient. Capital equipment
spending rose strongly in the June quarter, and imports point to
further gains despite higher freight costs and a weaker currency.
Confidence rebounded after the initial shock from the Middle

East conflict and has held up despite renewed oil price pressure.
Investment will remain uneven, but cash-rich agricultural
businesses are well placed, and computing equipment and
software are contributing to growth.
Inflation is the main complication. Consumer prices rose 3.7%
in the year to September and annual inflation is expected to
reach 4.3% in December. Higher global oil prices have played
an important role, but the problem is broader than fuel. Core

inflation measures remain around 3%, operating costs are
elevated and firms’ pricing intentions have strengthened.
Headline inflation should fall as energy prices ease and could
temporarily dip below 2% in 2028, but underlying inflation is
expected to decline much more slowly.
That persistence means the RBNZ will likely need to tighten
policy by more than it currently forecasts. The OCR is expected
to reach 3% by year-end, but we think the neutral rate may

be closer to 3.75%, in contrast to the RBNZ’s 3% to 3.5%
estimate. As growth strengthens and core inflation proves
sticky, we expect the OCR to peak at 4% in September 2027
and remain there for about a year. Medium- and long-term
interest rates are therefore likely to settle above the unusually
low levels seen in the decade before the pandemic.
Finally, fiscal conditions should improve as stronger nominal
activity and above-target inflation lift tax revenue. However,

spending pressures and higher debt-servicing costs will be
difficult to avoid. We expect the operating balance before
gains and losses, excluding ACC (OBEGALx), to return to
surplus in 2028/29, while net core Crown debt peaks near
43.7% of GDP in 2027/28. The unresolved election adds
uncertainty, however; the forecasts assume no major near-
term change in policy direction.
Turning to this week’s key economic news, RBNZ Governor
Anna Breman and fellow MPC member Prasanna Gai spoke

to audiences in New Zealand and Australia respectively. We
thought that Breman’s comments were reasonably balanced.
On the hawkish side, Breman noted that some indicators of
economic activity had been stronger than expected. Perhaps
of greater note, she also said that this month’s partial October
CPI data (discussed below) indicated that indirect impacts
from the fuel price shock might be growing following a more
muted than expected impact in the September quarter. On

the dovish side, she noted subdued domestic demand, which
she said was making it difficult for firms to pass on higher
costs. She also referred to weak labour market data (including
weak wage growth) and the stability seen in medium-term
inflation expectations (also discussed below). Following the
October review Breman had commented that the chances of a
December rate hike were “high”.
In our view, Gai’s commentary leaned more on the dovish
side. He reiterated his concerns about the implications for NZ

of a bursting of the AI bubble and described recent inflation
and inflation expectations data as “quiescent”. In response to
a question, he indicated that he considered that news since
the October meeting had validated his decision to vote for a
pause at that meeting. The general tone of Gai’s commentary
suggested it would not be a surprise if he voted to extend the
pause at next month’s meeting. Even if he were to vote for
a hike, it seems likely that Gai would regard this as simply a

tentative step towards the roughly 3.25% neutral rate that the
Bank projected in the September Monetary Policy Statement.
On the data front, the partial CPI data for October pointed
to weaker than expected food price inflation but firmer than
expected increases in travel costs. The latter was probably
responsible for Breman’s comments above, but given the
month-to-month volatility of this data we would be hesitant
to read too much into one month’s outcome. These opposing

impacts netted out and so the outcome was consistent
with our forecast for a lift in annual inflation to 4.3% in the
December quarter. Meanwhile, the RBNZ’s long-running
survey of professional forecasters reported a lift in year-ahead
inflation expectations. However, encouragingly, the closely
watched 2-year-ahead measure was little changed at 2.36%.
Moreover, 5-year-ahead and 10-year-ahead expectations –
which had been singled out for special attention by RBNZ

Chief Economist Conway in a recent speech – fell to 2.17%
and 2.13% respectively. In both cases, these were the
lowest readings in almost two years. Finally, encouragingly,
the BusinessNZ services PMI continued its gradual trend
improvement in October, rising to a 3-year high of 51.7.

### Page 7

We expect this positive momentum to have carried into Q3 
2026, with further solid gains in residential building, private 
non-residential building and private engineering construction, 
particularly electricity-related projects. Public infrastructure 
investment will offset some of this strength, with the drag that 
began last year continuing as major projects reach completion.
AUS: October CPI (%mth)
November 25, Last: 0.8%, Westpac f/c: 0.4%  
Mkt f/c: 0.4%, Range : 0.3 to 0.6
We expect the October CPI to rise 0.41%mth with the annual 
pace lifting from 4.3%yr to 4.8%yr, the fastest pace in the 
short history. Westpac expects this to be the peak in current 
acceleration in annual inflation. Our seasonally adjusted CPI 
estimate lifts 0.7%mth, a small step up from the 0.6% increase 
in September, highighting the seasonal softness in the month. 
Transport makes the largest contribution (+0.35ppts), almost 
entirely due to auto fuel, with smaller contributions from 
clothing & footwear (+0.06ppts), household goods & services 
(+0.05ppts) and meals out & takeaway (+0.04ppts). The 
main offsets are recreation & culture (–0.18ppts) with fruit & 
vegetables, utilities and others providing marginal offsets.
For more details please see “October CPI Preview“.  
AUS: October Trimmed Mean (%mth)
November 25, Last: 0.2%, Westpac f/c: 0.4% 
Mkt f/c: 0.4%, Range : 0.3 to 0.5
Our October Trimmed Mean estimate is 0.42%mth, a step up from 
0.3% in September, taking the annual pace to 3.7%yr from 3.6%yr 
and lifting the six month annualised pace to 4.2%yr, from 4.0%yr, 
which is also the fastest pace in the short history of the series. 
Rents and dwellings continue to underpin the TM estimates. 
We see risks as slightly tilted to the upside. Our estimates 
allow for a greater degree of price resetting in areas such as 
meals out & takeaway and personal and household services, 
but this adjustment proves broader than our assumptions. 
Fuel levy surcharges are another source of upside risk, 
including increases by Woolworths and Australia Post. The 
key downside risk remains housing, particularly new dwelling 
prices, which surprised to the downside in recent months.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
NZ: Q3 retail spending (%qtr)
Nov 23, Last: -0.5, Westpac: 1.4
The June quarter saw a larger than expected 0.5% fall in
retail spending, with high fuel prices squeezing households’
spending power. However, even with the drag from high
fuel prices, core spending (excl. fuels and vehicles) remained

resilient, rising by 0.7%. With fuel prices dropping through
much of the September quarter, we expect overall spending
levels will lift by 1.4%. Importantly, with lower fuel prices
putting money back into households’ pockets, that’s likely to
be underpinned by firm gains in core spending categories.
AUS: Q3 Private CAPEX (%qtr)
Nov 26, Last: –3.6%, WBC f/c: 10.0%
Mkt f/c: 4.5%, Range: 2.0% to 10.0%
Private capex declined 3.6%qtr in Q2 2026 as data centre-

related machinery and equipment investment pulled back
sharply. With nominal ADP equipment imports surging
83%qtr to another record high, we expect a sizeable rebound
in the Q3 figures. Buildings and structures should also find
support as the broader strength in private non-residential
approvals through the first half of 2026 works through the
pipeline. Overall, we expect private capex to grow a strong
10.0%qtr in Q3, with data centres again leading the charge.

Est 3 for 2026/27 capex plans, after our adjustment, is
consistent with impressive nominal growth of 17.9%yr and
real growth of 15.3%yr. We expect Est 4 unadjusted plans for
2026/27 to come in at $221bn, a 15.5% increase on a year ago,
indicative of the scale of investment yet to occur.

Chỉ số, nhóm ngành, khối ngoại và phái sinh

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX giảm điểm trong tuần qua, -32.53 điểm tương ứng -1.83%, với thanh khoản suy 
giảm. Linh kiện ô tô, Dầu khí và Công nghệ là các nhóm ngành có mức giảm rõ rệt nhất 
trong tuần. Khối ngoại duy trì bán ròng so với xu hướng của tuần trước với giá trị -634 tỷ 
đồng, tập trung ở các mã FPT, ACB và VPL; mặt khác, tự doanh đảo chiều bán ròng ở mức 
-609 tỷ đồng.

Ở một khía cạnh khác, khối ngoại đảo chiều bán ròng -5,829 HĐTL VN30F1M trong tuần 
qua và đang duy trì vị thế bán lũy kế -1,462 hợp đồng. Tự doanh duy trì mua ròng 722 hợp 
đồng nhưng đang duy trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng -5,278 hợp đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam đánh mất đà hồi phục của tuần trước, khi VN-Index quay đầu giảm khoảng 
1,8% về đóng cửa quanh 1.742 điểm, với phiên cuối tuần có thời điểm lùi sâu về sát 1.727 
điểm trước khi thu hẹp đà giảm. Dù mùa báo cáo KQKD quý 3 diễn ra tương đối tích cực, 
tuy nhiên các lo ngại về việc môi trường lãi suất cao vẫn duy trì trong bối cảnh căng thẳng 
Trung Đông leo thang đang đè nặng lên tâm lý nhà đầu tư. Kết quả kỳ họp của FED trong 
tuần qua nhìn chung phù hợp với kỳ vọng và không gây ra ảnh hưởng đáng kể. Điểm kém 
tích cực nhất là khối ngoại đã quay lại bán ròng với tổng giá trị khoảng 2.700 tỷ đồng 
trong tuần — đảo ngược diễn biến mua ròng cuối tuần trước, cho thấy đà rút vốn ngoại 
vẫn chưa thực sự dừng lại.

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX tuần qua hình thành mẫu nến giảm trên đồ thị tuần, nhưng chưa thoái lui quá 1 
nửa cây nến tăng của tuần trước, cùng thanh khoản được tiết giảm, hàm ý nhịp điều chỉnh 
vẫn trong phạm vi cho phép. Trên khung ngày, sau bốn phiên giằng co đầu tuần với thanh 
khoản thấp, chỉ số đã chịu áp lực sụt giảm mạnh hơn trong phiên cuối tuần, chớm xuống 
dưới đường tín hiệu chính giữa dải Bollinger, nhưng cho tín hiệu rút chân ngay sau đó, thể 
hiện áp lực bán tháo đã tạm thời được kiểm soát. Mặc dù rủi ro mở rộng thêm nhịp điều 
chỉnh vẫn còn để ngỏ, tuy nhiên khi xu hướng tăng ngắn hạn đang được bảo lưu, VNINDEX 
được kỳ vọng sẽ cho phản ứng hồi phục trở lại tại các điểm đỡ bên dưới.

(Ghi chú: NĐT có thể dựa theo nhận định xu hướng thị trường để tham khảo khuyến nghị 
hành động tương ứng trong bảng phía trên) 
Kháng cự xa: 1850 (+-15) 
Kháng cự gần: 1800 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ gần: 1720 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ xa: 1680 (+-15)

Danh mục đầu tư mẫu

Phương pháp tiếp cận: 
- Danh mục tập hợp các cổ phiếu tiềm năng nhất được KBSV 
lựa chọn và tái cơ cấu theo tháng. 
- Ngưỡng cắt lỗ cần được cân nhắc cho mỗi mã là -15%. 
- Danh mục giả định luôn duy trì tỷ trọng 100% là cổ phiếu với 
trọng số được phân bổ đều giữa các mã.

Lưu ý:  
- NĐT được khuyến nghị mua tại mức giá bằng hoặc thấp hơn 
giá khuyến nghị mua. 
- Lợi nhuận kỳ vọng được tính theo giá khuyến nghị mua. 
 So sánh hiệu suất với VN30 Index

trưởng 6%-8%/năm

### Page 1

CHIEF INVESTMENT OFFICE
Capital Market Outlook

### Page 2

2 of 8 November 16, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Inflation Sticky but Overheating Less Clear
Chief Investment Office, Macro Strategy Team
The disconnect between very strong recent growth with stubborn inflation above the Fed’s 2%
target on one hand and more moderate business surveys and labor-market indicators on the
other, has complicated the optimal Fed policy path and contributed to large swings in interest

rates. Indeed, the economy looks strong in several respects. Real GDP grew at a 3.4% annualized
pace in Q3, led by 3.8% real consumer spending growth, according to Bureau of Economic
Analysis (BEA), surging AI-related business investment, and a 16% annualized increase in real
federal government spending. As policy uncertainty eased from its tariff-related spike and growth
remained resilient, labor data have also improved. Job openings and quits rates have stabilized.

The October payrolls reports exceeded expectations. The unemployment rate, according to
Bureau of Labor Statistics (BLS), slipped to 4.1% from 4.5% in November 2025.
Stronger hiring alongside higher-than-expected October consumer price index (CPI) inflation revived
expectations for further Fed tightening, however, causing renewed sharp interest rate increases.
Yet, as discussed below, the recent strong growth reacceleration followed a period of softer, tariff-

shock-related economic conditions, and some of its driving forces are likely to moderate. Moreover,
inflation still appears disproportionately related to energy, tariffs, freight costs and increasing
AI-related electronics inflation. Tariffs have already started to fade, though, while diesel prices have
potential to decline once geopolitical conflicts resolve. In this context, there is a risk that higher
interest rates would not only overly restrain demand but would also delay investment in housing,

power generation and other capacity expansion needed to keep inflation in check.
Here are a few details underpinning this view:
• The strong Q3 real GDP growth acceleration followed just a 1.6% annualized pace over the preceding
three quarters, with consumer spending also up at a moderate 2.1% average rate. At about 2.1%
and 2.5% year-over-year (YoY) growth, respectively, neither GDP nor consumption growth appear
exceptional, according to BEA. The Chicago Fed National Activity Index, which aggregates information

from 85 economic indicators seems to concur—following three years of subtrend readings, it has
oscillated between slightly above- and below-trend growth territory for most of the year.
• Business surveys also suggest ongoing expansion but not booming growth. The National Federation
of Independent Business Small Business Optimism Index remains below its July level even as job
openings, hiring intentions, and plans to raise worker pay spiked in October. The October Institute

for Supply Management (ISM) manufacturing survey has remained a bright spot, with production and
employment numbers particularly robust. While the overall index is associated with roughly 2.7% real
GDP growth, according to the ISM, its services counterpart has lagged, with below-average readings
across new orders, production, and employment in October. As a result, the weighted average of the
two surveys remains way below levels seen in prior expansions (Exhibit 1), in line with the moderate

growth dynamics pervading the Fed’s October Beige Book, for example.
• According to BLS, hiring was unquestionably strong in October. Payrolls gained 165,000. Hiring in
previous months was revised up. The three-month average jobs growth shifted to 137,000 from just
28,000 in July. Unemployment dropped to 4.1%. Still, part of the improvement represents catch-up
from the hiring lull observed after the tariff shock and related spike in policy uncertainty (Exhibit 2).

Labor turnover remains low, with the quits rate unsurprisingly depressed given the sharp uptrend
in the average duration of unemployment, which increased in October to a five-year high (also a
historically high level).
• Labor supply has also surprised to the upside as employment strengthened, precluding excessive wage
growth outside specific areas of technically skilled labor scarcity. After cratering in the year following
the tariff shock, the labor force participation rate (LFPR) has meaningfully rebounded since July,

helping bring nearly 1.6 million people back looking for work, according to BLS. Indeed, the supply of
labor is partly endogenous to economic conditions—it rises when jobs availability and hiring improve,
and vice versa. Although the prime-age female LFPR is near recent records, a rebound in participation
rates for males age 20 to 54 toward their 1995 to 2000 highs would allow meaningful additional
employment growth before wage-related inflation pressures become an issue.

In itself, renewed hiring following the tariff-related hiatus therefore need not be inflationary. Higher
employment alongside rising labor force participation and solid productivity growth should help
contain unit labor costs. It would also help expand the supply side to better match resilient demand,
helping keep inflation in check and prolonging the expansion.
Investment Implications
We remain overweight Equities,
favor U.S. stocks and see potential

opportunities in broader market
participation and cyclical sectors.
Treasury yields appear attractive for
income generation and diversification
reasons.

### Page 3

3 of 8 November 16, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
• Several supports to recent growth appear unsustainable. Base effects and midterm elections
results suggest a likely lessened growth impetus from AI investment. Faster inventory
accumulation, which significantly boosted Q3 real GDP, cannot continue indefinitely. Also, real
consumer spending barely inched higher in September following an August spike, according to
BEA. Indeed, consumer spending has increasingly outpaced income growth this year, sharply

eroding saving out of income from 6.2% in January to 3.8% in September, limiting the scope
for spending to keep outpacing income. Finally, while Q3 earnings have continued to positively
surprise across most sectors, margins may have limited scope of further expansion from
historically elevated levels. Together, based on our analysis, these factors point toward slower
but still healthy economic growth around 2.3% ahead, according to BofA Global Research.

• According to the BLS, “core” CPI inflation remained faster than desired in October, with
evidence of some broadening. The retreat in Brent oil from roughly $135 to $95 per barrel in
recent weeks has been encouraging, though elevated diesel prices and freight costs are still
spilling into other prices. Also, shelter seems to have run its disinflation course, accounting
for about a third of the October “core” CPI increase as excess rental inventory got absorbed.

Housing-services inflation is now close to its prepandemic average, providing a fading
disinflation tailwind.
All in all, growth is likely to moderate ahead, the tariff effect on inflation is dissipating,
and lower diesel prices would materially relieve near-term inflation pressure. Yet a set
of more structural supply constraints and potential inflation pressures remains to be
addressed. Rising electricity costs, infrastructure bottlenecks, skilled-labor shortages,

an aging population, housing costs and supply limitations, a wealthy segment of the
population relatively insensitive to inflation, disruptive trade and industrial policies, and
the resource demands associated with the AI-investment boom suggest a multitude of
potential inflation pressures if accommodated by persistent large fiscal deficits, excess
credit growth, a wage-cost spiral or unmitigated wealth effects. This strengthens the
case for measures to boost productive capacity and policies to better align nominal

spending with potential supply, if inflation is to sustainably return to target through
increased supply rather than higher reliance on Fed-induced demand suppression.
Restrictive interest rates may in fact delay the very supply response needed to alleviate
some of these inflation pressures.

MARKET VIEW
From Earnings Strength to Earnings Durability
Kirsten Cabacungan, Vice President and Senior Investment Strategist
U.S. Equities have faced a series of crosscurrents in recent months. At the index level, stocks
have seemingly gone nowhere. The S&P 500 has yet to break out of the sideways trading
range that began in early June, rising less than 1% over that period. But that relatively flat

performance masks considerable movement beneath the surface. As enthusiasm around the
AI trade dominated through the summer, market leadership narrowed and breadth steadily
deteriorated. By the end of September, nearly 60% of S&P 500 constituents were more than
15% below their 52-week closing highs, suggesting a sizable correction was already unfolding
beneath the headline index. October and November brought another shift in leadership as
intensifying headwinds around the AI trade pressured mega-cap Technology stocks, while

previously lagging areas began to recover. The Philadelphia Semiconductor Index fell as much
as 38% from its mid-June peak through early November and remains roughly 31% below that
high despite a recent partial recovery.
Against that volatile market backdrop, corporate fundamentals have told a much steadier
story. With roughly 91% of S&P 500 companies having reported as of November 13, Q3
earnings-per-share (EPS) growth is tracking around 32% YoY according to FactSet, which,

if sustained, would mark the third consecutive quarter of earnings growth above 25% and
the eighth consecutive quarter of double-digit growth. The magnitude of earnings growth in
recent quarters is unusual this far into an economic expansion and more closely resembles
the earnings rebounds typically seen following recessions. In fact, outside of the recoveries
following the 2000 Dot-com bubble peak, 2008/2009 Global Financial Crisis and 2020
pandemic, Q2 marked the fastest pace of earnings growth across the last three decades

(Exhibit 2). The underlying results have also remained strong, with revenue growth firmly in
double-digit territory and the Q3 net profit margin tracking its second-highest level since
FactSet began measuring the metric in 2009.
Exhibit 2: Earnings Growth Resembles a Post-Recession Rebound.

Yet equity markets have shown surprisingly little response to that earnings strength. According to

BofA Global Research, companies beating expectations on both earnings and sales outperformed
the S&P 500 by just 90 basis points the following day, compared with a historical average of 1.4
percentage points. Companies missing on both have traded roughly in line with the market, versus
an average historical underperformance of 2.5 percentage points. In turn, the resulting performance
gap between beats and misses is the narrowest since Q4 2000 and Q4 2001, following the March

2000 peak in the Dot-com bubble.1 The disconnect seems to be less about the strength of current
results than the increasingly high bar investors have set for what comes next.
Some moderation from here is almost inevitable. An increasingly difficult YoY comparison
base sets a high hurdle after several quarters of exceptional growth. BofA Global Research
expects S&P 500 EPS growth to slow from a projected 33% in 2026 to 12% in 2027, while
1 BofA Global Research, “Earnings Tracker: Week 4: 2021 beat rate, 2001 price action,” November 6, 2026.

We continue to favor staying

market leadership becomes more

remain highly diversified and

### Page 5

5 of 8 November 16, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
bottom-up consensus remains somewhat more optimistic at 16%. That would represent a
meaningful deceleration, but it would not necessarily signal deterioration as double-digit
earnings growth would still leave the fundamental backdrop on solid footing.
The bigger question for investors though is what could turn that expected earnings growth
normalization into a sharper slowdown. Midterm election results have introduced a new source

of uncertainty about the AI investment cycle, as voters and newly elected Democratic governors
in several states at the center of the data center boom have supported greater restrictions on
development. That risk matters disproportionately for the earnings outlook, with Technology
and Energy accounting for roughly 75% of Q3 S&P 500 earnings growth (Exhibit 3A). BofA
Global Research estimates that a hypothetical 20% reduction in 2027 AI capital spending

could translate into 35% to 40% downside to AI semiconductor EPS and lower S&P 500 EPS
by roughly 7% to 8%, before accounting for secondary effects. So far, however, a slowdown
has yet to emerge in company guidance, with hyperscalers continuing to raise capital spending
plans. While recent market repricing has reflected some of that uncertainty, the durability of AI
investment and earnings remains an important factor in the broader earnings outlook.

For now, the conditions that typically accompany a broader earnings downturn remain largely
absent. Economic activity continues to expand at an above-trend pace, with the Atlanta Fed
GDPNow model tracking 3.5% annualized real gross domestic product growth for Q4. Consumer
spending also remains firm heading into the holiday season. Bank of America aggregated credit
and debit card spending per household rose 5.8% YoY in October, or 4.6% excluding gasoline.2

Although both measures moderated from September, they remain close to the strong growth
rates recorded since the spring. That demand resilience continues to show through corporate
earnings results. According to FactSet, 84% of S&P 500 companies have reported actual EPS
above mean estimates, well above the five-year average of 78%. And that strength extends well
beyond the largest contributors to index earnings growth, with nine of the 11 S&P 500 sectors

seeing 80% or more of reporting companies exceed consensus EPS estimates (Exhibit 3B) and
all sectors tracking positive earnings growth for 2026. While the earnings cycle may be moving
beyond its strongest phase, the broader backdrop does not yet point to a fundamental downturn.
For investors, a moderating earnings backdrop should place greater emphasis on where
earnings growth is coming from and how durable that growth ultimately proves. Greater

market dispersion should favor companies with healthy operating cash flows, strong balance
sheets and the ability to translate investment into sustainable returns. That applies particularly
to the AI buildout. A slower or more disciplined pace of capital spending would not necessarily
signal an end to the investment cycle but could help separate the companies and projects that
can ultimately deliver on their investment from those where expectations have moved too

far, too fast. That type of differentiation should become increasingly important as the market
moves into a more measured phase of the cycle.
